### Q1337. An animal that produces metabolic heat at a low level and relies on the environment to regulate its body temperature is called a(n):
A.) Endotherm
B.) Homeotherm
C.) Poikilotherm
D.) Ectotherm **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Ectotherms produce metabolic heat at a low level and rely more on heat derived from the environment to raise their body temperature.

### Q1338. The glomerular filtrate has a chemical composition most similar to:
A.) Whole blood
B.) Urine
C.) Distilled water
D.) Blood plasma **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The glomerular filtrate has a chemical composition similar to that of blood plasma, but it lacks larger molecules like proteins, red blood cells, and platelets.

### Q1339. The functional unit of the kidney is the:
A.) Renal pyramid
B.) Renal cortex
C.) Renal pelvis
D.) Nephron **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The nephron is the functional unit of the kidney, and it consists of a renal corpuscle and a renal tubule.

### Q1340. Why are urinary tract infections (UTIs) significantly more common in women than in men?
A.) The female urinary bladder is larger.
B.) Women have a lower urine pH.
C.) The male urinary tract produces antibacterial substances.
D.) The female urethra is much shorter. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The higher risk of UTIs in women is mostly due to the shortness of the female urethra (1.5 inches compared to 8 inches in men), which allows bacteria to be more easily transferred to the bladder.

### Q1341. A urine test shows a persistently acidic pH. This patient is at a higher risk for which type of kidney stone?
A.) Calcium phosphate
B.) Magnesium ammonium phosphate (Struvite)
C.) Calcium oxalate
D.) Uric acid **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — When urine is persistently acidic, there is a higher risk of forming uric acid type stones. Calcium phosphate stones tend to form in alkaline urine.

### Q1342. The thermoregulatory center of the human body, acting as a thermostat, is located in the:
A.) Cerebellum
B.) Medulla oblongata
C.) Cerebral cortex
D.) Hypothalamus **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The thermoregulatory centre in the human body is located in the hypothalamus, which acts as a thermostat, detecting the temperature of the blood passing through it.

### Q1343. The primary function that distinguishes juxtamedullary nephrons is their ability to:
A.) Filter large volumes of blood.
B.) Reabsorb most of the glucose.
C.) Actively secrete hydrogen ions.
D.) Produce highly concentrated urine. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Juxtamedullary nephrons have long loops that extend deep into the medulla. This makes juxtamedullary nephrons key for water retention when water is in short supply.

### Q1344. During peritoneal dialysis, where is the dialysate fluid introduced?
A.) Directly into the bloodstream via a catheter in the arm.
B.) Into the kidney itself via the renal pelvis.
C.) Into the urinary bladder.
D.) Into the abdominal cavity. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — In peritoneal dialysis, catheters are surgically inserted into the abdominal cavity, through which the dialysate enters and leaves.

### Q1345. The process where substances are moved from the filtrate back into the blood is called:
A.) Glomerular filtration
B.) Tubular secretion
C.) Micturition
D.) Tubular reabsorption **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Selective reabsorption is the process by which certain substances that have been filtered out of the blood are reabsorbed back into it.

### Q1346. Which of the following is NOT a cause of chronic renal failure?
A.) Diabetes mellitus
B.) High blood pressure (hypertension)
C.) Bacterial infection of the kidney (pyelonephritis)
D.) Severe hemorrhage (sudden blood loss) **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Severe hemorrhage due to trauma is a cause of acute renal failure. Chronic renal failure may be caused by bacterial infections, high blood pressure, and diabetes mellitus.

### Q1347. The funnel-shaped structure within the kidney that collects urine before it passes into the ureter is the:
A.) Renal medulla
B.) Hilum
C.) Fibrous capsule
D.) Renal pelvis **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The ureter begins as the funnel-shaped renal pelvis, which collects urine within the kidney.

### Q1348. The descending limb of the loop of Henle is primarily permeable to ______, while the ascending limb actively transports ______.
A.) Sodium chloride; water
B.) Glucose; urea
C.) Urea; water
D.) Water; sodium chloride **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Water passes out of the descending limb by osmosis, while the ascending limb actively transports sodium chloride out into the surrounding kidney interstitium.

### Q1349. Which structure carries urine from the urinary bladder to the outside of the body?
A.) Ureter
B.) Renal artery
C.) Collecting duct
D.) Urethra **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The urethra is a tube that carries urine from the urinary bladder to the outside of the body.

### Q1350. The main purpose of secreting hydrogen ions (H+) into the filtrate is to:
A.) Reabsorb more sodium ions.
B.) Conserve water.
C.) Eliminate toxins like drugs.
D.) Regulate the pH of the blood and urine. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The main purpose of secreting substances like hydrogen ions is to maintain the pH of the urine, which normally ranges from 4.8 to 7.5.

### Q1351. A homeotherm is an animal that:
A.) Allows its body temperature to fluctuate with the environment.
B.) Absorbs heat primarily from the external environment.
C.) Generates most of its body heat through metabolism.
D.) Maintains a fairly constant internal body temperature. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Homeotherms, like birds and mammals, are able to maintain a fairly constant body temperature by using physiological mechanisms.

### Q1352. All of the following are typically found in the glomerular filtrate EXCEPT:
A.) Glucose
B.) Sodium ions
C.) Urea
D.) Albumin (a large protein) **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Larger molecules like proteins, as well as red blood cells and platelets, are left behind in the blood and do not pass into the glomerular filtrate.

### Q1353. Hypercalciuria is a condition that directly contributes to the formation of which type of kidney stone?
A.) Uric acid stones
B.) Cystine stones
C.) Magnesium ammonium phosphate stones
D.) Calcium oxalate stones **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Increased calcium in the blood (hypercalcemia) causes high calcium in the urine (hypercalciuria), which along with high oxalate levels (hyperoxaluria) causes calcium oxalate stones.

### Q1354. Which part of the renal tubule is responsible for reabsorbing the majority of water, ions, and all organic nutrients like glucose?
A.) Loop of Henle
B.) Distal convoluted tubule
C.) Collecting duct
D.) Proximal convoluted tubule **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The proximal convoluted tubule is where the reabsorption of water, ions, and all organic nutrients occurs.

### Q1355. Shivering warms the body primarily through:
A.) Increased blood flow to the skin.
B.) Trapping an insulating layer of air.
C.) Decreasing the rate of sweat evaporation.
D.) Heat generated by spasmodic muscle contractions. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Shivering is the spasmodic contraction of muscles, and this contraction produces heat which helps to raise the body temperature.

### Q1356. What is the primary role of the urinary bladder?
A.) To filter waste products from the blood.
B.) To produce hormones that regulate blood pressure.
C.) To transport urine from the kidneys to the exterior.
D.) To serve as a temporary reservoir for urine. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The urinary bladder is a hollow and distensible muscular organ that serves as a urine reservoir.

### Q1357. An animal that excretes urea as its primary nitrogenous waste is described as:
A.) Ammonotelic
B.) Uricotelic
C.) Anhydrobiotic
D.) Ureotelic **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Animals which excrete urea as their major nitrogenous waste product are called ureotelic.

### Q1358. The tough, fibrous connective tissue layer that encloses and protects each kidney is called the:
A.) Renal cortex
B.) Peritoneum
C.) Renal pelvis
D.) Fibrous capsule **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — A fibrous connective tissue layer, called the fibrous capsule, encloses each kidney.

### Q1359. Besides excretion, the kidneys play a crucial osmoregulatory role by:
A.) Producing metabolic water from carbohydrates.
B.) Storing excess water in the renal pelvis.
C.) Secreting sweat to cool the body.
D.) Forming either concentrated or dilute urine depending on the body's hydration state. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — As osmoregulatory organs, the kidneys are concerned with the formation of diluted urine during flooding and concentrated urine during dehydration.

### Q1360. Which hormone, secreted by the posterior pituitary, promotes the reabsorption of water from the collecting ducts?
A.) Aldosterone
B.) Renin
C.) Erythropoietin (EPO)
D.) Anti-diuretic hormone (ADH) **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The movement of water out of the descending limb and collecting duct is promoted by anti-diuretic hormone (ADH), which is secreted from the posterior lobe of the pituitary.

### Q1361. The cone-shaped tissues within the renal medulla are called:
A.) Renal columns
B.) Nephrons
C.) Calyces
D.) Renal pyramids **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The renal medulla consists of a number of cone-shaped renal pyramids.

### Q1362. Piloerection helps to warm the body by:
A.) Increasing blood flow to the hair follicles.
B.) Generating heat through muscle contraction.
C.) Reflecting heat away from the skin.
D.) Trapping an insulating layer of air. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Piloerection, or the 'erection of skin hair', traps air in the erected hair, which acts as an insulator for heat.

### Q1363. A key factor in a successful kidney transplant is matching the donor and recipient's ABO blood group and which other factor?
A.) Rh factor
B.) Mitochondrial DNA
C.) Body Mass Index (BMI)
D.) Human Leucocyte Antigen (HLA) **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — ABO blood group compatibility is essential for a kidney transplant, and it is also usual to select donor kidneys on the basis of human leucocytes antigen (HLA) matching to improve graft survival.

### Q1364. In humans, the small amount of uric acid excreted daily is primarily a breakdown product of:
A.) Proteins
B.) Carbohydrates
C.) Fats (lipids)
D.) Nucleic acids **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Humans excrete small quantities of uric acid, but this is produced from the breakdown of nucleic acids, not proteins.

### Q1365. The final part of the nephron, which receives filtrate from several distal convoluted tubules, is the:
A.) Loop of Henle
B.) Proximal convoluted tubule
C.) Vasa recta
D.) Collecting duct **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The distal convoluted tubule is connected to the collecting duct, which receives filtrate from many nephrons.

### Q1366. A patient with end-stage renal disease (ESRD) might have a condition called uremia, which is characterized by:
A.) High levels of glucose in the blood.
B.) An abnormally low number of red blood cells.
C.) Excessive water retention leading to swelling (edema).
D.) The buildup of toxic nitrogenous wastes in the blood. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Chronic kidney failure can progress to end-stage renal disease (ESRD) and uremia, which is fatal unless treated with dialysis or a transplant.

### Q1367. Which of these is a behavioral, rather than physiological, response to thermoregulation?
A.) Sweating
B.) Shivering
C.) Vasodilation
D.) Putting on a jacket **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — A behavioral response, such as changing clothing, is voluntary and often corrects a temperature imbalance without the need for a physiological response like shivering or sweating.

### Q1368. The medial depression of the kidney where the renal artery, renal vein, and ureter connect is called the:
A.) Cortex
B.) Renal sinus
C.) Medulla
D.) Hilum **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The medial side of the kidney is concave, and the entrance to the hollow chamber (renal sinus) is termed the hilum, where the renal artery and nerves enter, and the renal vein and ureter exit.

### Q1369. In peritoneal dialysis, the exchange of waste products occurs across which membrane?
A.) A synthetic cellophane membrane in a dialyzer.
B.) The walls of the glomerular capillaries.
C.) The wall of the urinary bladder.
D.) The peritoneum. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Peritoneal dialysis involves the use of a natural membrane in the body, the peritoneum, which encloses the abdominal cavity.

### Q1370. The conversion of highly toxic ammonia to the less toxic urea occurs in which organ?
A.) Kidney
B.) Spleen
C.) Pancreas
D.) Liver **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The liver supports the excretory role of the kidney by producing ammonia, urea, and uric acid from the nitrogen of amino acids.

### Q1371. Which of the following would be a cause of acute, rather than chronic, renal failure?
A.) Long-term, poorly controlled diabetes mellitus.
B.) Gradual damage from years of high blood pressure.
C.) Slowly progressive inflammation of the glomeruli (nephritis).
D.) A sudden obstruction of the ureter by a large kidney stone. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Acute renal failure may be caused by an obstruction of the ureters, bladder, or urethra by a kidney stone. Diabetes and high blood pressure are causes of chronic renal failure.

### Q1372. The primary function of the Loop of Henle is to ______ in the renal medulla.
A.) filter the blood and produce filtrate.
B.) reabsorb all organic nutrients like glucose.
C.) secrete hydrogen ions and regulate pH.
D.) create a salt concentration gradient. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The formation of hypertonic (concentrated) urine is dependent on the reabsorption of water from the loop of Henle and collecting duct, achieved by the countercurrent multiplier mechanism.

### Q1373. Vasodilation helps to cool the body by:
A.) Slowing down blood flow to conserve energy.
B.) Diverting blood away from the skin surface to the core.
C.) Triggering the release of sweat from glands.
D.) Increasing blood flow to the skin. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Vasodilation is the expansion of blood capillaries beneath the skin, which increases blood flow and allows heat to be carried away from the blood through the skin.

### Q1374. Urea is approximately how many times less toxic than ammonia?
A.) 100 times
B.) 1,000 times
C.) 10,000 times
D.) 100,000 times **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Organisms invest energy to convert ammonia into urea, which is 100,000 times less toxic than ammonia.

### Q1375. The science concerned with the structure, function, and diseases of the kidney is called:
A.) Urology
B.) Endocrinology
C.) Hepatology
D.) Nephrology **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The science concerned with the structure, functions, and diseases of the kidneys is called nephrology.

### Q1376. Which hormone acts on the ascending limb of the loop of Henle to control the active transport of sodium chloride?
A.) ADH (Anti-diuretic hormone)
B.) Erythropoietin
C.) Insulin
D.) Aldosterone **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The movement of sodium chloride out of the ascending limb is controlled by the hormone aldosterone.

### Q1377. Which of the following best describes the path of filtrate through the nephron after leaving the Bowman's capsule?
A.) PCT -> DCT -> Loop of Henle -> Collecting Duct
B.) Loop of Henle -> PCT -> DCT -> Collecting Duct
C.) DCT -> Loop of Henle -> PCT -> Collecting Duct
D.) PCT -> Loop of Henle -> DCT -> Collecting Duct **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — The renal tubule leads away from the Bowman's capsule and becomes the proximal convoluted tubule (PCT), which then forms the loop of Henle, followed by the distal convoluted tubule (DCT), and finally connects to the collecting duct.

### Q1378. The PCNL procedure accesses a kidney stone:
A.) Via a scope through the urethra.
B.) By using external shock waves.
C.) Through a large open surgical incision.
D.) Via a small tube through the patient's back. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — In PCNL, a tube is inserted from the patient's back into the kidney to create a tract, through which a scope and equipment can be inserted to break up and remove the stone.

### Q1379. The main difference between poikilotherms and homeotherms is based on:
A.) The source of their body heat (internal vs. external).
B.) Whether they live in aquatic or terrestrial habitats.
C.) The type of nitrogenous waste they produce.
D.) The ability to maintain a constant internal body temperature. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Animals are classified as poikilotherms if they are unable to maintain their body temperature within narrow limits, and as homeotherms if they are able to maintain a fairly constant body temperature.

### Q1380. If a person's kidneys were failing, what would you expect to happen to the concentration of urea in their blood?
A.) It would decrease significantly.
B.) It would remain unchanged.
C.) It would be converted back to ammonia.
D.) It would increase significantly. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Kidney failure is a decline in kidney function, particularly the efficiency of the filtering process. This leads to a buildup of nitrogenous wastes like urea in the blood.

### Q1381. The expansion of blood capillaries just beneath the skin to increase heat loss is known as:
A.) Vasoconstriction
B.) Piloerection
C.) Evaporation
D.) Vasodilation **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Vasodilation is the expansion of blood capillaries which lie just beneath the epidermis of the skin, leading to more blood flow and subsequent heat loss.

### Q1382. A staghorn stone is a large kidney stone that is notable for its:
A.) Extremely smooth surface in the renal pelvis.
B.) Pure uric acid composition.
C.) Tendency to form only in acidic urine.
D.) Branching shape that fills the renal pelvis. **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — A branching stone that can fill the renal pelvis is called a staghorn stone.

### Q1383. Which of these animals would be classified as a homeothermic endotherm?
A.) A fish
B.) A lizard
C.) A frog
D.) A human **(answer)**
**Answer: D** — Homeotherms (maintaining constant temperature) include birds and mammals. Endotherms (producing their own heat) also include birds and mammals. Humans are mammals.
